- **runtime-benchmarking-of-computational-methods**: Use when comparing execution speed of two implementations of the same method suite (e.g., R vs Python decoupleR) on large simulated omics matrices, or when reproducing a scalability benchmark reported as ms/sample/regulator. - **runtime-benchmarking-of-feature-selection-methods**: Use when you need to compare wall-clock runtime of feature/marker-selection algorithms on the same datasets at an identical number of selected features, e.g. reproducing or extending the paper's Table 2 speed comparison. - **runtime-benchmarking-of-implementations**: Use when two implementations of the same decomposition (e.g. JIVE variants) must be compared for computational efficiency on identical data, and runtime/memory gains must be validated against equivalence of the fitted decomposition. - **runtime-benchmarking-of-tcr-models**: Use when comparing computational scalability of TCR-transcriptome integration methods (mvTCR, CoNGA, tessa) on datasets of varying size, or when deciding whether a model can scale to atlas-level (&gt;60k cell) immune profiling data.
